# homecookd: state of the project

Shared by every model and person working in the repo. Keep it true: every PR updates the sections it affects
and adds a line to the **Change log** at the bottom (see "Shared context protocol" in `AGENTS.md`). Where an
older note in `memory/` disagrees, this file wins.

## Repo

- GitHub: `calebwang26/hackathon-homecookd`. Local: `~/Documents/GitHub/hackathon-homecookd`. The older repo
  `calebwang26/homecookd` (formerly EDIBLE-venturehacks) is now `Homecooked-Old`.
- As of Oct 21, all 16 worktrees under `.worktrees/` are clean and fully merged
  into main, so they can be removed with `git worktree remove .worktrees/<name>` once Caleb agrees.
- Untracked files in the main checkout: `demo/*.png` (Expo Go QR codes and screenshots). These are Caleb's. Leave them alone.

## Live infrastructure

- **Supabase** project `edible`, ref `qeiednrjkiyzbolkepbk`. Edge functions:
  - `api` **v15** (last deployed Oct 20, by Salik, the CTO). Both Caleb's agents and Salik deploy it. Before you deploy,
    check with Caleb that Salik's work is merged and no deploy is in flight, so nobody overwrites the other's live routes.
  - `transcribe` v4.
  - The api has 10+ modules (`index.ts`, `retention.ts`, `plumbing.ts`, `time.ts`, `table.ts`, `recap.ts`, `cooking.ts`,
    `hearts.ts`, `tablePr.ts`, `recapPr.ts`, …). List `supabase/functions/api/` on main and deploy every file.
  - There's no `supabase` CLI or access token on this machine. Claude deployed through the Supabase MCP
    (`deploy_edge_function`, files inlined, esbuild type-stripped when too big). With Codex, either configure the
    Supabase MCP (`codex-mcp.toml`) or have Caleb run `supabase login` and deploy with the CLI.
- **Vercel** project `homecookd` → https://homecookd.app (fallback `edible-psi.vercel.app`). Root Directory must be
  `web`. A red Vercel check on a PR has usually meant that setting, not the code.
- **GCS** buckets `edible-media-*` (public, no listing) and `edible-uploads-*` (private), plus the Cloud Run upload signer. See `README.md`.
- **Hackathon (Oct 23–25), `docs/build/STACK.md`:**
  - **Convex** project "homecookd", preview deployment `hackathon` → https://grand-dragon-649.convex.cloud
    **No expiration** since Oct 25: previews are deleted 5 days after creation, and this one (created Oct 23) was set to
    go on Oct 28. Its `expiresAt` was cleared through the Convex management API, after a full backup with file storage
    (`npx convex export --include-file-storage`, saved outside the repo at `~/Documents/homecookd-backups/`). There's
    no prod deployment yet; moving to one would change the URL in the app, voice service, worker, signer, web and glasses VM.

- Oct 22 · #42 · Claude · `web/`: Next.js and eslint-config-next 16.4.0 → 16.4.2 for the security advisories GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw (critical: image optimization RCE via SVG), GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr (critical: `use cache` RCE) and GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r (high: Server Actions DoS). The live site is a static export with a custom image loader, no Server Actions and no `use cache`, so exposure was limited to local `next dev` servers. Nothing left open.

    2 of the 4 demo recipes; I re-sent them one at a time.
  - **Fix:** one sync per family at a time (`families.mossSync`). A sync sends every ready recipe and deletes stale
    docs. A change during a sync triggers one more pass, and a stuck sync stops blocking after 10 minutes.
    `indexRecipe` is replaced by `syncFamily`.

The header stays put with homie at its top right; small chips under the pot (Back · Repeat · Ask <cook>; no Next chip, the big "Next step" button does that) talk to homie, or act on their own when voice is off or homie doesn't answer in 1.5 s. The body's Ask pill is now the chip; the "waiting" line stays.
  - **Voice:** no mic button. In a dev build with the voice service, on a Convex dish, voice starts by itself once the "Let Mom see" sheet has its answer (`consentSettled`; a memorial cook has no sheet, `cookingSessionOn`). A tap on the pot turns the mic off or on (no moment; turning it on, homie says what the kid can say). `lib/live/voice.ts` handles say `kind`/`textEn`, clip `quote`/`quoteEn`, `timer`/`timer_done`, and sets the pot. The pot shrinks while the cook's own voice plays (store `shrunk`), and homie's next line waits until her clip or voice note ends (`setHold`). Stub in `lib/live/voiceStub.ts`.
  - **Half duplex** is `lib/live/mic`'s, for every caller: while homie is lit or speaking, anything plays on the shared player, or a step video plays, each frame is zeroed PCM of the same length, marked `quiet` (`halfDuplex`, `useHalfDuplex`; `lib/media` `videoPlaying`, `useVideoPlaying`, `audioNow`). `/record` sends the zeros; cook mode sends nothing for them (round 3, Cartesia credit fix A). So the help line, homie's lines and her clips never reach the service as the kid's words.
  - **Moments** (`components/cooking/CookMoments.tsx`): no buttons (Caleb, Oct 24; memory `homie-talks-no-bubble`). The read-aloud offer, a story nudge and "<cook> answered your question" show the glowing pot, the question, five listening bars, "Say “yes” or “no”" and "Tap anywhere to close". A spoken yes does it; a spoken no closes it and is kept on the phone; a tap anywhere is "not now" for that visit only (nothing saved). "Ask <cook> anything" has only the bars (the Ask chip opens the chat when voice is off or muted). A phone that can't hear the answer gets one button ("Read it to me", "Hear it", "Play it"). Whenever no voice line reads (Expo Go, web, or the line gave up or failed: `voice.unavailable`, `phoneReads`) the phone offers read-aloud itself and reads each step in homie's voice (`useReadSteps`). Kv `homie.readAloud`, `homie.heardAnswers`.
  - **Shared contract, app side** (STACK.md; the service's side is #42): `{ type: "moment", open, kind }` while "Grandma answered your question" (`answer`) or "Ask Grandma anything" (`ask`) is up, sent again on a new socket; `{ type: "text", text: "no" }` declines the read offer for a tap anywhere or a remembered no; `offer_closed` closes the offer's moment and keeps the answer when homie heard it first.
  - **Review fixes:** round 1: late `say_end`s (`endSay`), a clip drops homie's line, the echo answers a chip, the screen's step wins on `ready`, Repeat goes to homie only for read-aloud kids, no reading over her clip, leaving drops a line, "I made it" hangs up, the mic stops under another screen, `timerKey`. Round 2 (homie core review): the help tap only speaks (no moment, the "AI voice" label), `speak()` has one 8 s deadline over the Convex session and the request, a 12 s watchdog on 'lit' (`MAX_LIT_MS`, since lit holds the mic), the overlay is modal for VoiceOver while a moment is up, no blink when a moment closes, `useHomieSelect` (screens don't re-render on each mic level), "Translated from the cook" as the label's fallback. The cook header keeps the pot clear of "All steps".
  - **homie core API (for #35):** `HomieMoment` takes `options: []`, `bars`, `prompt`, `tapToClose` (`isCentredMoment`); store `dismissMoment`, `MOMENT_DISMISS`, `shrunk`, `MAX_LIT_MS`, `useHomieSelect`; logic `askFrame`, `HOMIE_ASK_SIZE`, `ASK_POT_GAP`, `ASK_SIDE`, `barHeights`, `BAR_MAX`, `SHRUNK_SCALE`, `halfDuplex`; hooks `useHalfDuplex` (`useAiSpeaking` stays, for what the screen shows); `components/homie/ListeningBars`. `lib/live/mic`: `useMicStream(onChunk, onLevel)` keeps its shape (`onLevel` is an API change to the core's mic.ts, now zeroed with the frames), and `stop()` returns a promise, as in #35.
  - **Tests:** `test-voice-ui.mjs` 27 pass (4 new: memorial, phone reads, app-only moments, the contract's messages; the yes/no test now follows #42's rules), `test-homie.mjs` 34 pass (3 new: the lit watchdog, half duplex). tsc, lint, every app test script and `test-profile-navigation` pass. Expo web at 390x844 (375, 393, 375x667, Reduce Motion), 34 checks: the pot clear of "All steps", the chips, the pill counting down with the ring, lit then speaking with "AI voice", a help tap with no moment, the shrink, every moment (modal), no blink after one, a chip's spring, a tap that closes without saving, "Read it to me" keeping yes.
  - **Open:** nothing run on an iPhone or against the live voice service yet. The server side is #42 (merged, not deployed): deploy it before an EAS Update brings this to phones, since the service that's live now takes a "no" as something to answer. The service keeps a kid's answer 3 h on its instance, so after a tap ("not now" for that visit on the phone) the next visit within 3 h gets no offer either. While an app moment is up, #42 holds only a whole yes or no ("yes", "yes please", "play it"): "yes, play it" plays her voice note on the phone but also reaches homie's AI. The phone's spoken yes and no now follow the service's own rules (`yesNo`: its `isYes`, and its whole-reply no, which wins). `TODO(caleb)`: the no-mic buttons "Hear it" and "Play it" (the spec names only "Read it to me"). The answer moment matches "my" questions by the asker's name or the ids this cook mode sent. Owned by #35 (core review): on parent Home the pot rests left of the name pill (`namePillWidth`) on the date line, so its header should keep the date clear of it; and chat hides homie instead of resting it left of the header's faces.

- Oct 24 · #47 · Claude · Fixes to #42 (from the verifier, five rounds): the kid's everyday yes and no. New `voice/src/yesNo.ts` is the one rule for the moment hold and the read-aloud offer, and follows the app's `yesNo` (#36): a yes word (yes, yeah, sure, of course) is a yes whatever follows ("Sure, read it.", "Yes, but how much ginger?"). "Okay", "ok", "alright" and "please" with more after them are a yes too ("Please do.", "Okay, let's do it."), and only they have the exception: with a command or a question after them it goes the usual way ("Okay, what's the next step?", "Okay, set a timer", "Please go back"). It adds the spoken no's with more after them ("No, not right now.", "Nah, I'm good.", "Don't.", "Oh, no thanks.", "Yeah, no."), and a no wins. "Not yet", "not really", "later", "skip it" and "I'm good" count only alone, so "Not really sure how much salt." and "I'm good at this." go the usual way, as do "No clue how much ginger.", "Don't forget the timer." and "Oh no, I burned it!". A command right after a no still runs (`afterNo`): "No, next step." declines the offer and moves the step silently, also with a moment up. Second round: decided "Yes, but how much ginger?" is a yes and "No, it's fine." a no. Every spoken no sends `offer_closed` (accepted false). Tests: voice 45 (8 yesNo tests, 3 new cook tests), typecheck passes. Live on fresh stub servers (real Ink 2, Sonic and Claude), with eight recorded lines (`sure-read-it`, `yeah-go-ahead`, `no-not-now`, `nah`, `dont`, and new `please-do`, `oh-no-thanks`, `no-next-step`): "Please do." accepted the offer and read step 1; "Oh, no thanks." sent offer_closed false 9 ms after the transcript, and a later "Yes." didn't turn reading on; "No, next step." sent offer_closed false, then go_to_step 2, silent and without Claude. With a moment up, "Please do." and "Oh, no thanks." were held, "No, next step." moved the step without Claude, and once the moment closed "Yes." accepted and read the step. Third round: any question, command or 3+ word statement after the no runs too ("No, I'm out of ginger. Can I skip it?", "No, the oil's too hot?", "No, go to step 3.", "No, play the video.", "No, tell me the story."): the offer is declined (offer_closed false) and the rest goes the fast path or to Claude, also with a moment up. More no isn't passed on ("No, thanks.", "No, not now.", "Nah, I'm good.", "No, maybe later."). Voice tests 48 (2 new yesNo tests, 1 new cook test). Live on a fresh stub server (real Ink 2, Sonic and Claude) with a new recorded line `no-ginger` during the read offer: offer_closed false 3 ms after the transcript, then Claude's hint about skipping the ginger and "I'll ask Grandma too." Fourth round: a "no need" no is only the no. The no now keeps the "no" of "no need", "no point" and "no reason", so "No, no need to read it.", "Nope, no need to read it out loud.", "Nah, no need for that.", "No, no need to play it." and "No need to read it." decline the offer and pass nothing on (before, Claude got "need to read it" and could read the step aloud), and "Nah, no need, next step." still moves the step. More ways to say only the no: "No, I'm all right.", "No, I got it.", "No, I've got it.", "No, I can do it.", "No, I'm good on that.", "Nah, I'm fine with that.", "No, you don't have to.", "No, I'm good, thank you so much." A mixed signal stays as said: "No, just read it." declines the offer and passes "read it" on to Claude. Voice tests 50 (2 new yesNo tests; the cook test for everyday no's has the four "no need" lines), typecheck passes. Live on a fresh stub server (real Ink 2 and Sonic), with a new recorded line `no-need` during the read offer: "No, no need to read it." sent offer_closed false 1 ms after the transcript, with no read-aloud and no Claude call. Fifth round (structural): Claude never gets the words after a no on their own, since cutting the no could flip what was left ("No point in reading it." became "in reading it", "No, no need at all to read it." became "at all to read it", and Claude could read the step). The no is handled first (offer_closed false, or the moment hold), then `afterNo` only says whether more was said: a plain command right after it still takes the fast path ("No, next step.", "Not now, set a timer for 5 minutes."; a timer only as the whole ask, so "No, don't set a timer…" goes to Claude), anything else goes to Claude as the kid's whole sentence with a note that the offer was just declined (`TurnAgent.turn`'s new `note`; with a moment up, that the no was the screen's), and more no is nothing. Reading can't come on from that turn (the decline is set first). A short reply after a no that isn't a known filler ("No? Fine.", "No, too salty.") now costs one Claude turn. Voice tests 51 (the afterNo tests rewritten; 2 new cook tests: the verifiers' ten replies, and a Claude that calls repeat_step anyway can't turn reading on), typecheck passes. Live on fresh stub servers (real Ink 2, Sonic and Claude) during the read offer: `no-ginger` sent offer_closed false 3 ms after the transcript, then Claude's hint about skipping the ginger and "I'll ask Grandma too.", nothing read aloud; `no-need` sent offer_closed false 2 ms after it, then nothing (no Claude call). As the app's text answer, "No point in reading it." and "Nah, no need really to read it." sent offer_closed false, and Claude, given the whole sentence and the note, said nothing.
  - **Open:** where the app's `yesNo` (#36) still differs. It takes a no only as the whole reply, so "No, not right now.", "Nah, I'm good." and "Don't." don't answer its moments (the service holds them, so nothing happens). It takes "okay" plus a question as a yes ("Okay, what's the next step?": the app's yes and the service's Claude turn both act), and "Yeah, no." as a yes. Changing that is #36's call; no app changes here. The kid app (#36, `app/src/lib/live/voiceLogic.ts` `wholeYesNo` / `thinksAfter`) still copies the old `yesOrNo`, so replies the service now holds during an app moment ("Sure, read it.", "No, not right now.", "Don't.") can leave the pot 'thinking' for up to 10 s (`THINKING_MAX_MS`) until #36 adopts these rules. Not deployed (`voice/deploy.sh`).

- Oct 24 · #50 · Claude · Voice hint latency and crash paths (from the Merge Agent's live test). Latency: main already did a hint and its ask to the cook in one tool (`give_hint` carries the question; the `[stub] question asked_cook` log line is the Convex write, not an `ask_cook` call). The slow part was a `find_moment` round trip whenever the search before Claude found nothing (always in the stub, whose index was deleted), plus 25-30-word hints. Now the message says the search found nothing and Claude doesn't search again, so a question is one call; the hint is under 15 words; the stub's `mossIndex` is `""` (no Moss); a missing family index isn't asked for again for 60 s; a search never holds a turn past its wait. Live, `CLAUDE_FAST=1`, merged with #49: hint first audio 1.7–2.4 s after the transcript (main 2.9–4.2 s), "I'll ask Grandma too." 8.0–9.7 s (main 11.6–14.1 s), 0.24–0.32 s after the hint ends; a plain ask 1.3–1.4 s (main 2.9–6.8 s). Standard speed and before/after tables in the PR. Crashes: each of these ended the whole process on main, and now doesn't: a text frame `null` on `/cook` or `/record`, a frame over 1 MiB, a text frame that isn't UTF-8 (no `'error'` listener on the socket), a `/speak` caller hanging up mid-body, a reset after a refused upgrade, and a question write failing while the hint plays (unhandled rejection). A failed action also left a `tool_use` with no `tool_result`, so Claude refused every later turn (400). New `voice/src/safety.ts`: each socket's errors are logged with its id (`cook-3`); an unhandled rejection never exits, an uncaught exception logs and exits for a clean Cloud Run restart (installed only when `server.ts` is the program). The Merge Agent's silent death wasn't reproduced (Moss stressed with 200 searches on missing indexes; no stack in its log, which fits the `pkill -f 'tsx src/server.ts'` another agent ran). Tests: voice 87 (new `agent.test.ts`, `hint.test.ts`, `server.test.ts`, which spawns the service and sends each crash input), typecheck passes; on main's code the new server and agent tests fail. - Oct 24 · (this PR) · Claude · **Stopping a recipe that's still being made works** (Caleb: "I'm clicking stop and it won't let me. It said couldn't stop, try again"). Cause (live logs): his Stop reached the upload signer, which refused it (403, three times) because that send's Convex job was never made (`family-recipe-ea10a0`: signer job at 7:20 PM, 0 of 1 files arrived, no Convex job), so `signer:owns` said no; the app showed the error. Why `jobs.create` never ran for it isn't known (Convex logs only reach back ~9 min). Fix: the x stops with no confirm; the row reads "Stopped" with Undo for 5 s (15 s with VoiceOver; a double tap on the x doesn't undo it), then the real stop runs (`components/parent/stops.ts`): Convex `jobs.cancel`, or new `jobs.cancelUpload` by the upload folder when the phone has no job id (after waiting for that upload's `jobs.create`), then the phone's upload; the signer's cancel is best-effort and can't fail the stop. Offline, the stop gives up after 15 s and the row comes back with "Check the connection". Leaving the app runs a waiting stop at once. A Convex job from a stopped upload that turns up later is stopped too, and a job stopped on another phone drops off. **Worker** (pipeline, Convex mode): `/worker/progress`, `/worker/release` and `/worker/published` answer 409 for a stopped job; `pipeline/worker.py` raises `JobCancelled`, saves nothing to Convex, deletes that upload's raw files (a glasses path: only its own file) and moves media it had just published to `trash/`; a storage error while cleaning up is logged, never fatal. `jobs.create` now refuses an upload folder another family's job already uses (new index `jobs.by_uploadPath`), so a stop can never delete another family's videos. Two review agents read the diff; their findings are fixed (worker crash on a failed purge, a publish window, a double tap undoing the stop, a swipe-kill losing the stop, an endless spinner offline, VoiceOver). Known and left: stopping during "Watching your video" still waits for that video's Gemini calls to finish (`process.py`), and pulling down Control Center within the 5 s runs the stop early. Tests: Convex 89 (new `cancel.test.ts`: every stage by id and by folder, the worker's 409s, finished/kid/other family refused, another family's folder, signer), `pipeline/test_publish.py` 13 (3 new), `app/scripts/test-parent-jobs.mjs` 7 (3 new), app `tsc` + lint, Expo web with a seeded pending recipe (x → Stopped/Undo → double tap ignored → Undo → x → gone after 5 s, no error). **Needs (Caleb's to run):** Convex deploy, then an EAS Update, then a restart of the pipeline worker from main (for the 409). Not yet done: a real stop on a phone (needs the EAS Update). Open: why that send's `jobs.create` never ran (a missing Convex job also means no recipe would ever be made from that video).
- Oct 24 · (this PR) · Claude · **Hotfix: the in-app recording crashed the app at "Done! Sending it to …"** (Caleb on TestFlight build 5; it also looked like a sign-out, see below). Root cause, mine from #69: `useInAppCamera.ts` `keep()` moved each finished clip from tmp to `documents/recordings/<id>/` with expo-file-system's `File.move`, which is async in SDK 57 (`AsyncFunction("move")`; `moveSync` is the sync one), and returned the old tmp path. So the done view asked iOS to upload a file that wasn't there (the signer's job file shows `size: 0`), and iOS aborts the app on an upload task for a missing file. Every in-app send on build 5 would crash. Fix: `moveSync` + return the new path; clips are numbered from part-01 (were part-00). New test in `test-recording-ui.mjs`: any un-awaited `.move(`/`.copy(` in `app/src` fails (it fails on the old line). The "logged out" look: the app reopened as the phone's owner, not the cook he'd picked (the picked person isn't kept across launches); the device stayed signed in. Sign-in hardening, a missing-file guard before uploads and resending a recording after a crash come in a separate PR (`claude/fix-record-crash`). Tests: app tsc, lint, test-recording-ui 31. **Needs (Caleb's call): an EAS Update to `production`** (JS only; build 5 has `moveSync`). Diagnosis by a crash-investigation subagent from Cloud Run, Supabase, GCS and Convex records; no iOS crash log was reachable.

- Oct 24 · (this PR, branch `claude/fix-record-crash`) · Claude · **After the build-5 crash: nobody signed out by a passing error, and an in-app recording the app dies on after "I'm done" is sent again** (follows #77's hotfix of the crash itself; Caleb on TestFlight build 5, recording `…8gyyb5`, 19:20 PT).
  - **The crash (fixed in #77):** `keep()` returned the old tmp path after the async `File.move()`, and the upload of the missing file aborted the app. Evidence: signer job file `jobs/family-recipe-ea10a0.json` has `size: 0`; the signer answered 200 at 19:20:28.65 and the `/record` socket dropped 0.3 s later with the screen still up; the pending send already in flight landed (19:20:30.3); `jobs.create` never did; no bytes reached the upload bucket. No iPhone crash log was reachable.
  - **The "log out":** the phone stayed signed in (its `devices` row from 13:22 was never revoked or replaced; the signer said "signed in as test cook" 23 s later). A relaunch opens the phone as its owner ("caleb", a kid), not the person picked last ("test cook"). Unchanged here: the phone still opens as usual.
  - **Sign-in hardening:** only a 401 with exactly "This phone was signed out." forgets the key (`isSignedOutAnswer`; before, any 401 on the key did, a gateway's too). A device-key 401/403 on `/family` keeps the cache. The keychain read is retried (`secretReadRetried`): failing while the app isn't in front (locked, a background launch) it waits and reads again on the next 'active'; failing in front it falls back to sign-in (never stuck). New keys are saved `AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK` (`saveKey` deletes the old item first: SecureStore's update only changes the value); keys from before keep "while unlocked" and still read (the query has no accessibility).
  - **Crash guards:** no upload task starts from a missing file: signer videos (`fileOnPhone`, before the signer and before each task), plate photo and Convex voice notes (`uploadFile`), postcard photos (`photos.ts`) and voice messages (`voice.ts`). `MISSING_VIDEO` now fits camera-roll picks too.
  - **Resend after the app dies (`lib/unsentRecordings.ts` + pure `lib/unsentLogic.ts`, `components/record/useResendUnsent.ts` in `app/_layout.tsx`):** a note from "I'm done" until Convex has the job AND every file is uploaded (`uploadFinished`; a swipe-away mid-upload cancels iOS's background transfer). Each launch and each return to the front, in the background and as that cook whoever is in (`convex.ts` keeps a Convex session per person now; `token/createJob/convexKidIds/cancelLiveJob/startUpload` take `as`). Convex says first (`recordingJob`: job, stage and error; a missing function or no answer = wait). Steps: wait (no answer; job `queued` or `preparing`, since the worker sets `preparing` on claim before it checks files and while it waits 45 s for them; failed for its own reason) / done (worker past its upload check: understanding/writing/indexing/done; stopped, error "Cancelled."; not among the family's 20 newest jobs; or this phone saw every file go) / create (upload done, only `jobs.create` missing) / redo (only `uploading`, the worker's own word that files are missing, and only an hour after "I'm done" unless this phone saw the upload fail, since iOS may still be finishing a background upload: `jobs.cancel` it, send again) / send (no job, upload unfinished) / gone (no videos on the phone: its job is left alone, the files may have arrived) / give-up (3 tries or 7 days; **silent**, videos stay, TODO(caleb) a way to send by hand). A redo can still cancel a job the worker claims in the moment between the read and `jobs.cancel` (after an hour of `uploading`; accepted). Sends the clips that are still there. Upload rows now carry `recording_id`, so a resend removes the recording's old rows first, and Stop/clear on Home drops the note by job or recording (re-read before each step, so a Stop mid-resend wins).

- Oct 24 · (this PR) · Claude · **Every send gets its Convex job, or says it couldn't** (Caleb: "find out why that send's jobs.create never ran, and fix it so a send can't silently end up with no Convex job"). Why it never ran: #77/#80 already found it, build 5's in-app camera crash right after the signer answered (`family-recipe-ea10a0`, 19:20 PT), before `jobs.create`. The gap left: `jobs.create` ran once, fire-and-forget; a failure or a crash at that moment left a send with no job forever (no recipe, the row just "Writing it down"), and #80's resend only covers in-app recordings on the next launch. Now: Convex `jobs.create` is idempotent per upload folder (returns the folder's job; a stopped one stays stopped). Each upload row keeps what `jobs.create` needs (`JobMeta.job_fields`); the home card asks again until Convex lists the job (`sendDraft.ensureJob`, `components/record/jobRetry.ts`: at once, then 30 s, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 60 min), and after the last try the row says "We couldn't start this recipe. Tap to send it again." A refused call (a kid Convex won't take, the language) asks again with less: without kids and language, then (not for a recording, whose job must stay linked to it) the folder alone; "signed out" isn't a refusal, the next try asks with everything. An in-app recording's row that this run of the app didn't send is left to #80's resend (one owner, never two jobs for one recording). Rows from older builds have no `job_fields` and aren't retried. A review subagent read the diff; its four findings are fixed (the two retry paths racing after a relaunch, signed-out dropping the kids, an unlinked job from a recording, a false "couldn't start" on a status-file row). Tests: Convex 90 (idempotent create, a stopped job stays stopped; #64's test now uses one folder per job), `test-parent-jobs.mjs` 8 (retry rules), app `tsc` + lint, Expo web (the failure row with its x). **Needs (Caleb's to run):** Convex deploy (idempotent create) before the EAS Update: with today's Convex a retry after a lost answer would make a second recipe.

- Oct 24 · (this PR) · Claude · **homie waits for the cook's answers at the end of a recording** (Caleb: "it doesn't wait for me to answer at all … you have to give time for the person to actually respond"; memory `homie-waits-for-answers`).
  - **Root cause:** `askAndWait` took her first speech-to-text turn end (any pause: Ink 2 ended "…until it looks" / "right." as two turns in a live run; Gemini Live 1.5 s after a final) as the whole answer, even an empty one, and asked the next line at once, half duplex, so homie talked over her, stopped hearing her, and the rest of her sentence answered the next question or the opt-in. The 15 s cap also cut long answers.
  - **Now** (`voice/src/record.ts` `listen`, `untilQuiet`): no wrap-up line starts while she talks (≤ 30 s). The card's tap counts from the `ask` on; her spoken turns from when the question played. An answer is every turn until no new words for 3.5 s (Ink 2, ink-whisper) or 4 s (Gemini Live) with no turn open; mic sound, or empty turns (kitchen noise), hold it ≤ 4 s with no words; 100 s cap per answer (under the app's 120 s stall). "not now" (the card's tap, `{type:"not_now"}`, or her first words) stops the line and skips only that question (`not_now`). Quiet 5 s after a question played → "Still there, Rajani Aaji? Take your time. I'm listening." (skipped if her words came meanwhile); 5 s more → `drop` + "That's okay, Rajani Aaji. No need to answer today. All good, let's finish up." and nothing more asked (`mem.letOff`: no end question, no opt-in, which stays unset; the plate photo still; goodbye). A speech-to-text that showed nothing, or errored since: a plain 15 s wait, no nudge or let-off. New `lines.ts` lines `stillThere`, `letOff` (en + Marathi, TODO(caleb) native check), made once per wrap-up, said and saved as `reaction`: no Convex or app change.
  - **Review fixes (independent reviewer):** every wait and the `said` spans count from when the phone plays a line (only once it has all of it: say_end + 250 ms `PLAYER_LOAD_MS` + its length; before, from the first chunk, ~1 s early); a tap during the line now skips instead of being dropped; "not now" plus more speech isn't saved as an answer; one collector across both waits (her words during the nudge count); empty Ink turns no longer hold forever (a 160 s probe now 4.2 s); a live question's card fades 10 s after a cough again; answers save `endS` at her last words; the mic's loudest level is logged per wait (`wrap-up mic:`). Live questions are otherwise unchanged; an empty turn no longer answers one.
  - **Second review:** a spoken turn answers a wrap-up question only if its words came after the question played (`Answer.played`): the mic is muted under homie's line, so an afterthought ("and a little salt") or family ("No, beta, come and eat") ending under it no longer answers the next question or sets the opt-in to no; taps still count from the ask. The mute now ends at say_end + the line's length (the phone sends silence while it plays; the old +300 ms on top of the 250 ms guess could cut a quick "yes"); hush() uses its own `playingUntil`. The let-off only follows a "still there?" she heard: a wait that noise held to its cap is waited again (at most 3 rounds) before the nudge. The noise cap's trade-off (a real turn starting inside a run of empty Ink turns gets no hold until its first words, ~0.3 s) is noted at `NOISE_MS`.
  - **Tests:** voice 200 pass (26 new in `record.test.ts`; the fake Sonic streams 5 pieces), typecheck; both reviews' probes now behave (afterthought: no answer, "still there?" 5 s after the question played; family talk: opt-in unset until her own "yes"). Live (local `VOICE_STUB=1 HOMIE_DEMO=1`, real Claude, Ink 2, Gemini Live, Sonic; `SCENARIO=long|silent|late` in `scripts/test-record.ts`, which models the phone's playback), on this head, all PASS: long slow answer (3 clips, 2.5 s and 3 s pauses, ~15 s) in Marathi (3 turns joined, next line 5.2 s after her last word; 5.4 s in two earlier runs) and English (3 turns, 4.0 s; 3.9–4.1 s earlier); silence in both (nudge 5.0 s after the question played, let-off 5.0 s after the nudge, no opt-in, goodbye). Earlier runs with 3 s / 3.5 s gaps also passed the late answer (2 s after the nudge: no let-off) and `CAMERA=1`, but one English long run cut the 3 s pause, hence 3.5 s / 4 s.
  - **Open:** `VOICE_RMS` (400 ≈ -38 dBFS) is from WAV test audio peaking at -14 to -18 dBFS; it needs phone data (a cook 1–2 m from a phone mic with no AGC may be -35 to -45 dBFS): read the `wrap-up mic:` logs from the first phone runs. Not tried on a phone. A live (while filming) answer is still saved as its first turn only. **Needs a voice deploy (Caleb's call).**

- Oct 25 · #85 · Claude · Caleb: "homie will talk and give me instructions, but then when i click into the video after homie stops talking, it'll be muted." Cause: after a step line the mic listens for 8 s, and while the recorder runs, iOS stays in PlayAndRecord / VoiceChat (audio-studio), which turns media playback right down. Nothing put the session back after the recorder stopped. Fix:
  - `voiceLogic` `micWanted` has a new `video` input: no recorder while any video plays. Its frames were zeroed anyway, and the window's count already pauses then. It starts again after the video.
  - `lib/live/mic` `stop()` (and unmount) hands the session back to `PLAYBACK_AUDIO_MODE` (`lib/media`, the same mode `_layout` sets at launch), except after a camera start.
  - Tests: test-voice-ui 70 (micWanted with a video; the stop puts playback back; the launch mode and the restored mode are one), all app scripts, tsc, lint. Web build: the step video plays unmuted at volume 1.
  - Not verifiable off a device: the iOS session behaviour itself. Needs an EAS Update (JS only).

- Oct 25 · #88 · Claude · Cook mode tap vs. hold, and "whatever was tapped last has the sound" (Caleb, Oct 25; memory `homie-tap-hold-and-audio-handoff`). Two builder subagents in worktrees, each checked by an independent reviewer, with every finding fixed.
  - **Hold** (about 400 ms, with a haptic): "Ask <cook> anything" with the cook's real name. The video fades and pauses first, and the mic is held on (`MicHold` 'ask').
    - In voice, the kid's sentence goes straight to the cook (`askCook`, no Claude; `askWords`). A yes-led question counts. A command still runs. "Never mind" / "no thanks" get "Okay." and close. A filler, or under 3 words, is ignored. Devanagari goes as it is. One ask per moment.
    - It closes 25 s after the kid last talked (never mid-sentence), or 2 s after the mic goes away.
    - The cook on her own dish, or no voice line: the step is read, or the chat opens.
  - **Tap:** homie on. Any video fades (150 ms) and pauses. The app sends `{type:"tap"}`, and voice says the current step's short line from the cache (+1 ms live), then homie listens. A tap again turns it off.
  - **Playing a video while homie talks or listens:** homie fades out and turns off (`hush`). The video waits for the handoff before it plays: `lib/media` `setVideoGate` waits up to 400 ms while the recorder stops and iOS goes back to playback (#85), because audio-studio deactivates the session on stop. So the video isn't halted, then nudged.
    - Who started a play is recorded on the playhead: a kid tap vs her clip (`appPlays`).
    - While the kid's video plays, late answers and a late `play_clip` are dropped. Unprompted lines still wait.
  - **Tests:** app tsc, lint, all scripts (test-voice-ui 80, test-homie 47, test-media new fade and gate cases); voice 205; Convex tsc.
    - Live (real Ink, Claude and Sonic, Marathi greeting, English kid): step flip +1 ms; tap during a video → the step line +1 ms; "Yeah, how long do I fry the onions?" in the ask moment → asked_cook +1 ms, no Claude; "Never mind." → "Okay."; a roommate line → quiet.
    - Web: the video fade and pause on a tap, play at volume 1, `kid` flags.
  - **Not tried on a phone:** the iOS session order, the up-to-400 ms wait before the video sounds, the hold timing and haptic.
  - **Needs:** a voice deploy BEFORE the EAS Update; otherwise a tap lights the pot for 2 s with no line and the kid's first words are lost. No Convex change.

- Oct 25 · (this PR) · Claude · **Front-camera clips filmed in the app are no longer stored upside down** (found by the pipeline session: `family-recipe-7f1ee1/01.mp4` has display-matrix rotation 90 where back-camera clips have -90, and its frames show the cook's face inverted). Cause, from #79: `CameraCard` passed `isMirrored={false}` to VisionCamera 4.7.3 for both cameras. iOS mirrors front-camera frames itself (connection auto-mirroring), and VisionCamera only takes that over (`videoOutput.isMirrored = true`) and turns the video's rotation 180° to match when mirroring is on (`CameraSession+Configuration.swift` step 2.2), so with `false` the frames stayed mirrored on the wrong axis and the clip read upside down. Fix: no `isMirrored` prop, so VisionCamera's own default (front mirrored, back not), the path its orientation code is written for. Front clips and the plate photo are now upright and mirrored like a selfie, as the preview (a packet's words read reversed in front clips). New test in `test-recording-ui.mjs`: `CameraCard` never sets `isMirrored` (it fails with the old line). Tests: app tsc 0, lint, every script (recording-ui 62). JS only: an EAS Update covers build 5 and the coming build 6. Not seen on a device. Open: front clips already uploaded stay upside down (a pipeline re-encode could turn them); if a front clip's text must read the right way, the pipeline could un-mirror front clips, but the app doesn't say which clips were front yet.

Only a validated uuid goes into the SQL; a profile in another family is refused. Skipped when the profile id isn't a uuid (seed/test people). Tests: new `convex/accountPurge.test.ts` 6 (vitest 120/120), convex tsc 0, new `pipeline/test_account_purge.py` 18, test_prompts 12, test_publish 15; the generated SQL was also run against `supabase/migrations/` in PGlite (seeded two families: only hers changed, a second run is a no-op, another family refused).   - **1. The cook's language.** A job's language is the uploader's profile language (unset = English) unless Record's pre-record sheet or homie's recording gives one (`convex/jobs.ts`); Poha went up from Salik's profile, so it was tagged `en`. Not a code bug, so the fix is a guard: an English transcript that looks forced (`doubtful_english`: over 40% loops, under 40 words a minute, Cartesia's "foreign" marker) gets a check (`detect_language`): the same 90 s sample transcribed in English and the 13 cook languages (~630 credits), and Sonnet picks the one that reads as real speech, by the words, never the script (`LANGUAGE_PROMPT`). When it's sure, the recording is transcribed in that language (`tagged_language`, `language_note`), and `process.run` switches the manifest (so the extraction prompt and the recipe say it). Unsure or failing: English, with a warning. Live: Poha tagged English (83% loops, 24 words a minute) came back Marathi; Sonnet noted Hindi read as speech too and picked Marathi by its vocabulary. Swordfish (en) triggered nothing.
  - **2. Cleaner audio.** 16 kHz mono, and every pause over 1.5 s cut to 0.3 s at each edge, a pause being quieter than the recording's own quiet level (10th percentile of 100 ms windows) + 3.6 dB; a time map puts every word back on voice.m4a's clock. Poha's glasses mic never drops below about -28 dB (a fixed -40 dB found nothing). Measured on Poha against Gemini's exact Marathi (share of Gemini's words heard in each step's seconds, 3 runs each): untouched 0.26, +2.5 dB 0.27, +3.2 0.32, **+3.6 0.36** (220 of 632 s cut, loops ~675 to ~280), +4.2 0.33, +5.7 0.29 (cuts speech). Normalising the loudness made it worse (0.23), so it's out. Swordfish: 0.97 before and after (its pauses have music under them: nothing cut). Fixed while testing: digital silence counted as -120 dB, not skipped (or a mostly silent file would cut its speech).
  - **3. The proofread** (`TRANSCRIPT_PROOFREAD=claude`, off by default; `CLAUDE_PROOFREAD_MODEL`, default Opus; non-English only): Claude corrects misheard words line by line (`PROOFREAD_PROMPT`: spelling only, amounts as heard, no ingredient swaps, no words moved between lines), on lines that end only at pauses (so मिर|ची isn't split), kept as `proofread` beside the raw words; `spoken_lines()` gives it to the extraction and align. Guards: a line it rewrites (`kept_sounds` < 0.45; a story line once became another line's words, 0.14) or lengthens a lot keeps its own words. Poha, 7 facts checked (mustard seeds, salt, cumin powder not chili, no added amount, green chili not chips, the mother's-poha story, coconut): first prompt 3/7 (it wrote chili powder for cumin, "about half" a bowl, chips); tightened prompt: Haiku 5-6/7, Sonnet 5/7 (chili powder every run), **Opus 7/7, 6/7, 7/7** (~$0.11 for 10.5 min, ~5k output tokens). Match with Gemini's Marathi: 0.36 to ~0.57.

Swordfish unchanged (English: no proofread).

- Oct 26 · (this PR) · Claude · **Repair tool for the upside-down front clips** (`pipeline/fix_upside_down.py`, test `test_fix_upside_down.py` 10/10). Read-only check of the uploads bucket (ffprobe over ranged HTTPS) and Convex: **6 clips** in 5 jobs are in-app front-camera clips with rotation 90, all filmed Oct 25: `family-recipe-d0a31c/01.mp4` and `02.mp4` (10:31 UTC, test family; its `03.mp4` is a back clip after a flip and is upright), `f4ad78/01.mp4`, `6ff35b/01.mp4`, `9363d8/01.mp4`, `7f1ee1/01.mp4` (Salik's family, 17:38–18:45). **No recipe is affected:** every one of those jobs failed (no steps / cancelled), their recipes (`jx723nh6…`, `jx7fxq39…`, `jx73y47e…`, `jx78tssn…`, `jx7393ym…`) are `failed` with no media, and the media bucket has no folder for them. ef4d81 is back-camera and fine. **What the stored picture is (evidence, high confidence):** the real scene turned 180°, *not* mirrored: shirt text in 9363d8 ("the brighter days…") and the Cal Hacks lanyard in 6ff35b read correctly once turned 180° and backwards when flipped top-to-bottom. So the pixels are right and only the rotation tag is off: the repair rewrites the tag 90 → -90 with a stream copy (video and audio packets byte-identical, creation_time kept; checked on 9363d8 and in the test). `--mirror` re-encodes mirrored instead, only if a real post-#104 front clip turns out selfie-mirrored. **Open, for the app chat:** by VisionCamera's code (#104 only turns the tag 180° and sets the same mirroring iOS already applied), a post-#104 front clip should be upright and **not** mirrored, unlike the memory's "mirrored like a selfie"; if iOS does *not* auto-mirror data outputs it would still be upside down. Check one front clip filmed on build 6 before running `--write`. Detection is only a list of candidates: the app films with `outputOrientation="device"`, so a back clip filmed with the phone upside down also reads rotation 90 (and a landscape front clip doesn't), so `--write` repairs only clips named with `--clip PATH` / `--clips FILE`, each checked by eye with `--try` first (d0a31c's two clips are dark, so they can't be). A display matrix with a flip in it is refused (it would still say rotation 90). `--write` repairs each listed clip locally and checks it, then backs up the original to `backup/<path>` in the uploads bucket and replaces it (only if unchanged meanwhile; metadata and cache-control kept); a failing clip is reported and the next one goes on. It never touches Convex, the media bucket or recipes. **Needs Caleb's OK to run --write**; with no recipe made from these clips it's optional. The 14-day lifecycle rule deletes the clips ~Nov 8 only if `--write` never runs: a rewritten clip and its new backup start a fresh 14 days.

- Oct 26 · (this PR) · Claude · Weak languages, the two cheap fixes (Caleb: "Do the two cheap fixes: proofread only the languages it helps, and stronger loop handling for music/noise. Then rerun the eval."). (1) `transcribe.retry_loops`: every stretch that looped (from the kept word before a loop to the one after it; `loop_spans`), every 4 s or more with no words (`silent_spans`), and the new `drop_babble` loops (12 words with 3 or fewer different ones) are sent to Cartesia again in pieces of up to 30 s with a second of context, through `afftdn=nf=-25`; a piece's words replace only its own stretch, and only when they are more and not mostly loops (`splice`); any failure leaves the first transcript. Probe: one looped 40 s of the Punjabi clip went from 1% of her words to 29%; the filter on the whole file gained 1 point. (2) `PROOFREAD_LANGUAGES` = hi, gu, ta, te, pa: it cost zh, es, tl, ur, ar 2-4 points. Eval, all 13 fresh (`--fresh`, new): heard (what the recipe uses) pa 0.26 → 0.47, ta 0.36 → 0.46, gu 0.26 → 0.32, ko 0.39 → 0.44, ar 0.69 → 0.75, ur 0.85 → 0.91, es 0.93 → 0.97, no language lower; ingredients pa 0.77 → 0.83, ko 0.47 → 0.60, ar 0.90 → 0.95; tl lost salt this run (1.00 → 0.92). $4.57 → $4.84 for all 13. Tests: `test_transcribe` 21 (6 new: babble, loop spans, silent spans and pieces, splice, the retry end to end on the original clock, the proofread languages); all pipeline suites pass. - Oct 28 · (this PR) · Claude · **The language eval is cheap by default** (Caleb, after Oct 26's $47 Claude bill, mostly testing: "implement all of these"). `pipeline/eval_languages.py` now needs `--only <langs>` or `--all`; runs each clip's first 5 minutes (`--minutes`; recipe ids `eval-<key>-<N>m`, so old 10-minute caches aren't mixed in); sends every Claude call through the Message Batches API at half price (new `CLAUDE_BATCH=1` in `pipeline/claude.py`, set only by the eval, never the worker: one-request batches, structured output via `transform_schema`, no server-side fallbacks since Batches refuse them, cancelled and sent the normal way after `CLAUDE_BATCH_WAIT_S`, default 30 min; `process.Usage` costs batched calls at batch prices and records `batch`); runs 4 clips at once while they wait; stops before passing `--max-usd` (default $3; `Budget`, a fixed + per-minute estimate per clip); reuses instead of re-running (`--rescore`, new `--rerun` keeps Cartesia's transcript, `--fresh` only for transcription changes); and counts the judge's two calls in the clip's cost (`spent_usd` per row). Results files are now `<date-time>.json`. Live check: Telugu, 5 min, all 10 calls batched, $0.22 (was $0.37), the same scores (heard 0.36 → 0.80 proofread, ingredients 0.96); each batched call waited about 2 min, so a clip takes ~15 min and all 13 about an hour for ~$2.60 (was ~$5). Tests: test_claude 12 (2 new: the batch request and price, the slow and failed batch), test_eval_languages 8 (2 new), transcribe 21, seen 6, shorten 10, cover 17, order 16, prompts 13, publish 15, coverage 16, account_purge 19, extract_claude 7. - Oct 31 · (this PR) · Claude (Pipeline & recipes) · **Two worker bugs AWS backend read in the code, both confirmed and fixed.** (1) At the spend cap `handle_convex` marked the job `failed` with "It will start on its own, nothing to do", but Convex has no way back from `failed` (only `release` re-queues), so the cook waited forever behind a Remove button. It now calls `convex.release`: the job waits as `uploading`, comes back every 30 s and runs by itself once `MAX_SPEND_USD` is raised (read from .env on each check). A pass at the cap reads no bucket: `_over_cap` runs before the upload check, with the video length remembered from the one pass that had to measure it. No words changed and no Convex deploy. **Open:** while paused the row reads "Still sending the video" (flickering with "Getting your videos ready"), so it's not a dead end but not the true reason; a real paused line needs a Convex field, app words and Caleb's OK. Nothing alerts anyone at the cap yet (a log line only); `aws-migration`'s queue worker already reports one. If the cap is never raised, the raw videos' 14-day lifecycle ends it with the missing-upload message. (2) A cook's stop raised `JobCancelled` inside `process.run`'s pool, whose exit waited for every queued extraction. New `process.report_extractions` drops the ones not started (`cancel_futures`); running ones still finish, because the worker deletes the folder right after. That only helps past 4 segments (over 1 hour of recording, ~$0.15 a segment saved), so a new check right after the transcript also stops a job before any frames go to Claude. Not stopped: a running extraction, the proofread and the writer each finish their current call. Tests: test_publish 16 (1 new), test_extract_claude 9 (2 new), prompts 13, claude 12, transcribe 21, seen 6, shorten 10, cover 17, order 16, coverage 16, account_purge 21, eval_languages 8. **Needs:** a worker restart from main (no worker is running on the Mac right now). The cap counts the running checkout's own `data/out`: on Oct 31, `worktrees/worker-main` $0.05, the main checkout $10.53 of the $15.

- Nov 3 · #328, for 1.1 · Claude (App screens & recording) · **`scripts/safe-eas-update.sh` on main: the AWS switch only on aws-staging** (follow-up to #327; App Store, web & builds found the gap). An EAS Update doesn't read eas.json's build env, so a bare `eas update` would ship a switch-off bundle to the aws-staging build, or a switch-on one to staging. For `aws-staging` the script sets the switch and staging's URL for the export and checks the bundle holds that address. For any other channel it refuses the switch in the shell or `app/.env`, and refuses a bundle holding that address. It always refuses `production`. Exports run with `--clear`: in testing, Metro's cache carried a switch-on bundle into a switch-off export, and the check caught it. The key scan and family-code check are in `scripts/check-app-export.mjs`, with the same shapes as aws-migration's `check-merge-safe.mjs --export`. aws-migration has an older `safe-eas-update.sh`; the merge into main should keep this one. AGENTS.md's update commands now use the script. AWS-PROOFS section 5 notes that the build lands in TestFlight under 1.0.0 (a 1.1.0 bump would change the runtime on every channel: the Master's call) and that the Convex and voice URLs carried over are unused while the switch is on and homie is off. Tested: `--check-only` passes on aws-staging and staging; production and a switch-on shell on staging are refused.
